### 2026-09-02 — Dashboard: `HouseholdsPage`'s own location filter had the same mobile-location bug

- Flagged as the same root cause as the payment-cycle wizard fix above, explicitly asked to fix here too: `HouseholdsPage.vue`'s State/County/Location/Village filters (and its "Village" table column's name lookup) were sourced from `GET_STATES`/`GET_COUNTIES`/`GET_LOCATIONS`/`GET_VILLAGES` -- the anchor's curated geo catalogue, not what's actually stored on `households.state_code`/`county_code`/`payam_code`/`boma_code` for a mobile manual-entry registration.
- **Fix:** `loadGeo()` now calls the `GET_HOUSEHOLD_LOCATIONS` endpoint added for the payroll wizard (no backend change needed here -- it already existed) for all four levels, passing `targetAnchorId` (System Admin's selected anchor, mirroring the existing `GET_ORGANIZATIONS` call right below it in the same function) rather than an organisation scope, since this page filters across every organisation in scope at once rather than one chosen organisation like the wizard does. `watch(selectedAnchorId, ...)` already called `loadGeo()` on anchor change, so no new watcher was needed.
- **Verification:** `npm run build` (`vue-tsc -b && vite build`) clean. Not live-verified against a real mobile-manually-typed household in this session.

### 2026-09-02 — Real cause of "Invalid or expired verification code": a dropped `payments.payment_cycle` column, not the OTP itself

- **Reported symptom:** generating a payment cycle still failed with `{"responseCode":"999","responseMessage":"Invalid or expired verification code"}` immediately after entering a code that was, by the user's own account, well inside the 5-minute TTL -- ruling out the expiry/trim theory from the earlier fix.
- **Diagnosis:** queried `otp_verifications` directly (`sqlcmd` against the remote SQL Server) -- the user's two most recent `PAYROLL_GENERATE` rows both showed `verified=1, attempts=0`, i.e. the OTP check itself *succeeded* both times. Cross-checked `backend/logs/2026-09-02/ERRORS/ERRORS-2026-09-02.log` and found, at the exact same timestamps, `com.biopay.services.Payroll | onDbError | Fail. {number=207 ... message='Invalid column name ''payment_cycle''.'}`.
- **Root cause:** `Payroll.java`'s `createLineItems()` (called only *after* `otpService.verify()` already marked the code single-use/consumed) inserts into `payments.payment_cycle` -- a column migration `031_legacy_cleanup.sql` dropped outright as a legacy duplicate of `payments.cycle`, without anyone updating this leftover reference. That INSERT throws, the whole `pool.withTransaction(...)` rolls back (so no cycle is ever created, silently), and the maker sees a generic failure. Because the OTP was already marked `verified=1` on that same first attempt, retrying "Confirm & Generate" with the very same (still-fresh, un-expired) code then correctly-but-confusingly reports "Invalid or expired" -- the code is not expired, it's already spent by the attempt that failed for the real, unrelated reason.
- **Fix:** `Payroll.java` `createLineItems()` -- dropped `payment_cycle` from the `INSERT INTO payments (...)` column list and its duplicated `@p4` placeholder in the `SELECT`; `cycle` (kept) already carries the same cycle code. Grepped the rest of the backend for any other reference to the dropped column -- none; every other `payment_cycle` hit is the distinct `payment_cycles` table, unaffected.
- **Verification:** `mvn compile` clean. Rebuilt the jar (`mvn package -DskipTests`) and restarted the local backend process again (PID 3628 -> new PID) so the fix is live; startup log confirmed every verticle, including `Payroll`, deployed cleanly. Not yet confirmed end-to-end with a real "Confirm & Generate" click against a freshly requested code -- next payment-cycle attempt should now either succeed outright or, if it doesn't, use "Resend code" first since any prior attempt's code is already consumed once OTP-verified regardless of outcome.
- Stopped that backend process again at the user's explicit request afterward, ending the local test session.

### 2026-09-02 — Anchor-generated payment cycles no longer need a separate approval step; fixed a table-layout CSS bug on Payment Cycles

- **Reported symptom (maker-checker):** an Anchor Administrator generating a payment cycle for one of their own organisations got `"The maker of a payroll cycle cannot also approve it"` when trying to approve it -- correctly requested clarification of the actual desired rule: an anchor generating for their own org shouldn't need a *separate* approval at all, since the anchor already is the approving authority; only a System-Admin-generated cycle should still require that organisation's own anchor to approve it (a genuinely different person/authority).
- **Root cause:** `Payroll.java`'s `generate()` always inserted the new cycle as `PENDING_APPROVAL` regardless of who made it, and `approve()` unconditionally blocks the maker from also being the checker (`TenantScope.isAnchorAdministrator` vs `isSystemOwner` was never consulted to tell these two "maker" cases apart).
- **Fix:** `generate()` now checks `TenantScope.isAnchorAdministrator(payload)` (true Anchor Administrator, not the System Admin) -- when true, the `INSERT INTO payment_cycles` lands directly as `APPROVED` with `checker_id`/`checker_at` self-set to the same maker (skipping `PENDING_APPROVAL` entirely), and the newly-created `payments` line items are marked `approved=1` in the same transaction, mirroring exactly what `approve()` itself would have done. A System-Admin-made cycle is unaffected and still lands `PENDING_APPROVAL` for that organisation's own anchor to approve. `GENERATE_PAYROLL`'s reply now carries `autoApproved`; `PayrollGeneratePage.vue`'s success toast reflects it ("...generated and approved" vs "...generated and pending approval").
- **Reported symptom (broken table):** a screenshot of the Payment Cycles list showed the Actions column's "Approve" button visually cut off, overflowing past the card's right border instead of the table scrolling horizontally within it.
- **Root cause:** `ui-fixes.css`'s existing rule for keeping multi-button action cells from wrapping (`.v-data-table td:has(> .v-btn + .v-btn)`) set `display: flex` on the `<td>` itself. A table cell that isn't `display: table-cell` stops participating in the table's native column-width/row-layout algorithm, so the browser can no longer size the row -- or the `v-table__wrapper`'s horizontal-scroll extent -- around that cell's real rendered width, and its content spills out past the table/card instead of scrolling with it.
- **Fix:** replaced `display: flex; gap: 2px` with a plain `margin-inline-end: 4px` on every non-last button inside such a cell (`:not(:last-child)`), keeping `white-space: nowrap` on the cell itself but leaving its `display` untouched. Deliberately without `!important`, so pages that already space their action buttons with Vuetify's own `!important`-carrying `mr-1` utility class (e.g. `PayrollPage.vue`) are unaffected, while pages that had none (e.g. `HouseholdsPage.vue`'s icon-only view/review/delete buttons, which relied entirely on the old flex `gap`) get spacing back.
- **Verification:** `mvn compile` (backend) and `npm run build` (`vue-tsc -b && vite build`, frontend) both clean. Backend jar rebuilt (`mvn package -DskipTests`) but the local backend process was left stopped per the user's preceding explicit "stop the backend" request -- needs a restart before either fix can be tested live. Neither fix was verified in-browser in this session (no login credentials available to the assistant); the CSS diagnosis rests on the well-established table-layout behavior of a non-`table-cell` `display` value on a `<td>`, not a live repro.

### 2026-09-02 — Payment Cycles: actions column redesigned (the margin-only CSS fix wasn't enough) and the View dialog now shows real household detail

- **User feedback:** the CSS-only fix above wasn't enough -- a follow-up screenshot still showed the Actions column's buttons cut off. Also asked for the "View" dialog to actually list each household with more detail, and for the page to "look good" generally. Routed through the `impeccable` skill (`node .claude/skills/impeccable/scripts/context.mjs`, then the mechanical detector) rather than another ad hoc patch, given this had become a real design request, not just a bug report.
- **Actions column, root cause of round two:** the real problem was never just the `display:flex` CSS -- it was four full-text buttons (**Approve**, **Reject**, **Disburse**, **Review**) plus an icon button, all needing to fit one cell in a 9-column table, on a page that (uniquely among this app's list pages) can show up to four simultaneous actions depending on role and status. Every other list page (Households, Vouchers, Officers, ...) only ever uses compact icon buttons in its Actions column.
- **Fix:** converted every action in `PayrollPage.vue`'s Actions column to an icon-only `v-btn` (`mdi-check-circle-outline` Approve/success, `mdi-close-circle-outline` Reject/error, `mdi-clipboard-text-search-outline` Review, `mdi-cash-fast` Disburse/secondary, `mdi-delete-outline` Delete/error, keeping the existing `mdi-eye-outline` View) wrapped in a `v-tooltip` naming the action, each still carrying its original `:aria-label` and `v-if` condition unchanged -- same visibility rules, same permissions, same click handlers, just the established icon-button language this app already uses everywhere else instead of an ad hoc set of wide text buttons. Wrapped the buttons in a local `.actions-cell` flex `<div>` *inside* the `<td>` (not on the `<td>` itself), which gets the same "don't wrap, small gap" behavior the earlier `ui-fixes.css` rule wanted without the table-layout-breaking mistake of making the cell itself non-`table-cell`.
- **View dialog:** widened (`max-width` 640 -> 820) and rebuilt around the household-level data `GET_PAYROLL` already returned but the dialog never displayed -- added **Village** (`bomaCode`) and **Gender** columns, and made each household's name a clickable link (new `viewHousehold()`) straight to its own household detail page (`household-detail` route, the same one `HouseholdsPage.vue` links to), so "more data about the households" means the real record, not a duplicated summary. Replaced the single run-on summary sentence with a small labelled `dt`/`dd` stat strip (households, currency+rate, amount out, amount in) styled from `DESIGN.md`'s existing label/value type scale, and added an explicit empty state ("No households in this cycle") for a cycle with none.
- **Design-system conformance:** added a `.num-cell { font-variant-numeric: tabular-nums }` class and applied it to every amount/count/rate cell in both the main table and the dialog, per `DESIGN.md`'s "Data Stays Still" rule (not previously applied on this page). Kept the flat, border-not-shadow card language and the existing teal/orange/success/error palette throughout; no new colors, radii, or component patterns introduced.
- **Verification:** `npm run build` (`vue-tsc -b && vite build`) clean; the Impeccable mechanical detector (`detect.mjs --json frontend/src/pages/PayrollPage.vue`) returned `[]`. Not verified live in a browser (no login credentials available to the assistant) -- the frontend dev server was left running throughout, so the change reaches it via HMR on next page load. Backend untouched this round; still stopped from the prior "stop the backend" request.

### 2026-09-02 — New Actions-column icons above were invisible: `mdi-subset.css` is a hand-maintained icon allowlist, not the full MDI font

- **Reported symptom (screenshot):** the redesigned icon-only Approve/Reject/Disburse/Review/Delete buttons rendered as plain flat-colored blobs with no glyph inside -- only the eye (View) icon and, partially, the green Approve checkmark were visible; Reject, Disburse, Review, and Delete were blank.
- **Root cause:** `frontend/src/plugins/vuetify.ts` imports `@/styles/mdi-subset.css`, not `@mdi/font`'s full ~800 kB icon sheet -- this app only ships a hand-curated subset of MDI glyphs, and that file's own 2026-08-22 comment says exactly this: "any newly-used icon needs its rule added here too or it silently renders no glyph (invisible icon buttons)." The new icon names from the previous redesign (`mdi-close-circle-outline`, `mdi-cash-fast`, `mdi-clipboard-text-search-outline`, `mdi-delete-outline`) all exist in the full `@mdi/font` package (verified against `node_modules/@mdi/font/css/materialdesignicons.css`, which is why they looked like reasonable choices) but were never in this app's actual runtime subset -- so their `<i class="mdi-...">` rendered as an empty box with only the button's own tonal background color showing, exactly matching "they just look like colors."
- **Fix:** added the four missing icons' exact glyph codepoints (pulled from the full MDI CSS: `\F015A`, `\F185C`, `\F1623`, `\F09E7`) to `mdi-subset.css`, following the file's existing one-line-per-icon convention -- kept the original semantically-best icon choices rather than substituting already-covered but less fitting ones. Cross-checked every `mdi-*` class used across every page touched this session (`PayrollPage.vue`, `PayrollGeneratePage.vue`, `HouseholdsPage.vue`) against the subset file to confirm nothing else was silently missing.
- **Verification:** `npm run build` clean. Not verified live in a browser (no login credentials available to the assistant); confirmed by direct string-matching every icon class each touched page references against the subset file's now-updated selector list -- 1 match each, no gaps. Frontend dev server left running throughout, so this reaches it via HMR on next page load.

### 2026-09-02 — Payment Cycles table: unset column widths let long content squeeze Actions off the visible edge and clip the Status chip

- **Reported symptom (screenshot):** with icons now rendering, the Delete button sat past the table/card's visible edge, and a separate crop showed the `PENDING_APPROVAL` status chip's text clipped mid-word ("PENDING_APPROVA") while shorter statuses (`APPROVED`, `DISBURSED`) rendered fine.
- **Root cause:** none of `PayrollPage.vue`'s `headers` had a `width`, so `v-data-table` (Vuetify 4.1.8) auto-sized every column from its own content/header text and let the browser's table layout compress columns to fit -- the longest status value (`PENDING_APPROVAL`) and the two-date `Period` column (already wrapping onto three lines even before this session's other fixes) were exactly the kind of long content that gets squeezed by that algorithm, and a squeezed cell clips rather than reflows. The Actions column, having no reserved width of its own, ended up being whatever was left over after the other columns claimed space -- on a long enough row, that pushed it (and its now-compact but still real icon buttons) past the visible card edge.
- **Fix:** added an explicit `width` (and `nowrap: true` where content must not wrap: Cycle, Organization, Period, Status, Actions) to every header in `PayrollPage.vue`'s `headers` array -- Vuetify applies each directly as that column's own inline style (verified against `VDataTableColumn.js`'s source, which maps a header's `width`/`nowrap` straight onto the cell). The table is now exactly as wide as its columns need (roughly 1340px total) and relies on `v-table__wrapper`'s existing `overflow-x: auto` to scroll horizontally on a narrower viewport, rather than silently compressing whichever column has the least claim to space.
- **Verification:** `npm run build` clean; Impeccable detector (`detect.mjs --json frontend/src/pages/PayrollPage.vue`) returned `[]`. Not verified live in a browser (no login credentials available to the assistant) -- widths were chosen from the actual longest real values in each column (`PAYROLL-20260902-E42BC5`, `2026-09-02 – 2026-09-30`, `PENDING_APPROVAL`) rather than guessed, but should be spot-checked against a real render. Frontend dev server left running throughout; reaches it via HMR on next page load.

### 2026-09-02 — Payment Cycles table: made the horizontal scroll (needed to reach Actions) actually visible

- **Ask:** now that the table correctly scrolls horizontally to reach the Actions column instead of squeezing it off-edge, give that scroll a visible scrollbar so people know to scroll rather than assuming the row simply ends.
- **Why it wasn't visible already:** `frontend/src/style.css` hides scrollbars everywhere in this app by design (`scrollbar-width: none` / `::-webkit-scrollbar { display: none }` on `*`, with the comment "the default OS/browser scrollbar reads as visual clutter against this UI") -- so `v-table__wrapper`'s own `overflow-x: auto` was working the whole time, just invisibly. This app already has exactly one deliberate exception to that global rule: `DefaultLayout.vue`'s `.dashboard-main` (the app's main vertical scroll container) re-enables a thin neutral scrollbar, matching `DESIGN.md`'s own stated rule ("a thin neutral scrollbar so long pages remain discoverable and operable by wheel, touch, keyboard, or drag").
- **Fix:** gave the cycles `v-data-table` a `class="cycles-table"` and mirrored `.dashboard-main`'s exact override (`scrollbar-width: thin`, `scrollbar-color: #94a3b8 transparent`, matching webkit track/thumb rules, just `height: 9px` instead of `width: 9px` since this one scrolls horizontally) onto `:deep(.v-table__wrapper)` -- same visible-scrollbar language already established elsewhere in the app, not a new pattern.
- **Verification:** `npm run build` clean; Impeccable detector returned `[]`. Not verified live in a browser (no login credentials available to the assistant). Frontend dev server left running throughout; reaches it via HMR on next page load.

### 2026-09-02 — View dialog showed a calm "No households in this cycle" when the real problem was a failed request

- **Reported symptom (screenshot):** opening View on a cycle showed the app-wide error toast "The BioPay server took too long to respond" -- the local backend process has been stopped since an earlier explicit "stop the backend" request and was never restarted -- but the dialog body underneath still calmly said "No households in this cycle," for a cycle that does have households.
- **Root cause:** `openView()`'s `catch` block only fired the toast; `viewItems` stayed at its reset `[]` either way, so the template's `v-else-if="!viewItems.length"` empty state couldn't tell "fetched fine, genuinely zero rows" apart from "the fetch itself failed." The toast auto-dismisses, but the misleading calm empty state stayed behind it.
- **Fix:** added a `viewError` ref, set in `openView()`'s catch block (and reset on every fresh open). The dialog now shows a distinct red "Couldn't load this cycle's households" alert with an inline **Retry** button (re-calls `openView` on the same target) ahead of the existing genuine-empty-state alert, so a failed fetch reads as a failure, not as data.
- **Separately:** the backend still needs restarting for View (and every other dashboard action) to work at all right now -- it was left stopped per the user's own prior request rather than restarted without being asked again.
- **Verification:** `npm run build` clean; Impeccable detector returned `[]`. Not verified live in a browser (no login credentials available to the assistant, and the backend that would need to be running for a real test is currently stopped). Frontend dev server left running throughout; reaches it via HMR on next page load.

### 2026-09-02 — Real cause of View's "took too long to respond": a BIT column crashing `paymentSummary()`, not the backend being down

- **User restarted the backend themselves and still got the same timeout on View.** Confirmed the new process (PID 15468, started 18:24) really was up and instantly answering requests (`curl` to the auth-gated endpoint got a prompt 401, not a hang) -- so "backend not running" was ruled out and the real cause had to be inside the specific `GET_PAYROLL` request itself.
- **Root cause, found via the (stale but revealing) error log from the assistant's own earlier restart:** `SEVERE: Unhandled exception / java.lang.NumberFormatException: For input string: "false"`, thrown from `Rows.intVal()` via `Payroll.intSafe()` via `paymentSummary()`, while building the response for every payment line item. `payments.rejected` is a SQL Server `BIT NOT NULL DEFAULT 0` column (added by `021_payroll_currency.sql`); the Vert.x MSSQL client returns a `BIT` value as a Java `Boolean`, not a `Number`, so `Rows.intVal()`'s fallback path (`Integer.parseInt(value.toString())`) tried to parse the literal string `"false"` and threw. Because this happened *inside* a Vert.x future's `.onSuccess()` callback with nothing catching it, the exception was merely logged -- `message.reply(...)` was never reached, so the eventBus request for `GET_PAYROLL` got no reply at all and sat until the ~20s reply timeout, which is exactly what the frontend's "took too long to respond" message means. Since `rejected` defaults to `0`/`false` on every row, this fired on essentially every cycle's View, not just one.
- **Fix:** `Rows.intVal()` now handles `Boolean` explicitly (`true`/`false` -> `1`/`0`) alongside the existing `Number` path, before falling back to `Integer.parseInt`. This is a shared utility used well beyond `Payroll.java`, so the fix covers any other BIT column read the same generic way, not just `rejected`.
- **Verification:** `mvn compile` clean. Rebuilt the jar (`mvn package -DskipTests`) and restarted the backend process the user had started themselves (PID 15468 -> new PID) so the fix is live; startup log confirmed every verticle, including `Payroll`, deployed cleanly with no errors this time. Not yet confirmed with a real "View" click against a live cycle in a browser (no login credentials available to the assistant) -- next click should now return instantly instead of timing out.

### 2026-09-07 - Email and OTP approval paths

- Added approval notifications for the two current approval workflows. A payment cycle that enters `PENDING_APPROVAL` emails every eligible anchor approver except its maker; a newly registered household emails eligible organisation and anchor reviewers. The normal create response now reports whether an eligible recipient was notified without rolling back the completed registration/cycle if mail delivery is unavailable.
- Added a branded, public `/approval` review page. The email link opens the request details first and requires a deliberate **Approve** button press; simply opening/scanning the email URL never changes data. Used, expired, superseded, rejected and unavailable requests lead the approver back to the dashboard OTP path.
- Added migration `040_email_approval_requests.sql` and applied it to the configured database. Approval links use high-entropy tokens, store only their SHA-256 hashes, expire after 24 hours by default (`APPROVAL_LINK_TTL_HOURS`, clamped to 1-168), and become unusable across every recipient after one approval. Confirmation rechecks that the named approver is active, still has the required permission, and still belongs to the request's organisation/anchor before changing the record.
- Email approval runs in one database transaction: payroll approval updates the cycle and its non-rejected payment rows together; household approval only accepts pending/legacy-checked records. The maker cannot approve their own pending payment cycle.
- Kept dashboard approval as the second path and clarified its UI. Approvers can request a six-digit OTP inside the payment-cycle review dialog. Payroll OTP verification is now bound to the exact cycle code, preventing a code requested for one cycle from approving another.
- Updated the transactional email template with escaped request content, a clear reference/summary panel, a single-use expiry notice and an explicit dashboard OTP alternative. Updated the backend example environment to the actual local frontend origin (`127.0.0.1:5175`).
- Verification: all 22 frontend tests passed; the production frontend build passed; all 31 backend tests passed with 3 opt-in live-database tests skipped; the Impeccable detector returned `[]`. Migration verification returned `table=ready, rows=0`. The rebuilt backend is running locally (PID 6648), and the public approval endpoint returned the expected safe invalid-link response without requiring authentication. The invalid/expired state was visually checked in the browser at desktop width. No real approval email was sent during verification.

### 2026-09-07 - Mobile payment fingerprint scanner ANR and crash fix

- Found the source-level cause of the payment scan screen reporting **App not responding**: `FingerprintVerifyActivity` called the blocking Morpho `open()` sequence on Android's UI thread. On the 6.42 hardware path that sequence can wait for the Peripheral Management AIDL service, power-cycle and boot the embedded sensor, retry USB enumeration for several seconds, then open the device. The screen could not draw or process input during that work.
- Moved payment scanner connection and USB startup to a dedicated single-thread executor. The verification screen now remains responsive and displays **Connecting to fingerprint scanner…** while initialization runs; the live verification starts on the UI thread only after the device opens successfully.
- Hardened cancellation and activity teardown. A cancel or destroyed screen marks the session as ending, queues scanner cancellation/close behind any in-progress connection, and ignores late progress, match, no-match or error callbacks so they cannot change the payment after the user has left.
- Wrapped the vendor SDK verification worker in both MorphoSmart 6.42 and 6.15 adapters with a `Throwable` boundary. A malformed template, native-library error or unchecked vendor exception is logged and returned through the normal red verification-failure result instead of killing the Android process. Vendor error text is preserved in the prominent failure message when available.
- Verification: all 23 unit tests passed for both MorphoSmart 6.42 and 6.15 variants; Android lint passed for both variants; both configured debug APKs assembled successfully; `git diff --check` passed. No Android device was connected during this pass, so the rebuilt 6.42 APK still needs the payment fingerprint path exercised once on the target tablet to confirm the device-specific runtime behavior and capture a log if its vendor firmware reports another error.

### 2026-09-08 - Multi-finger hand-picker capture + non-verification household/alternate photo

**Request (user going offline, no back-and-forth available -- proceeding on best-judgment decisions below, to be confirmed later):** During household registration, capture more than one fingerprint per person, with a visual hand representation (left/right hands, individual fingers) so the officer taps a specific finger (e.g. left thumb) before scanning it, sees that finger turn green once captured, and can finish with only one finger captured -- the rest capturable later. Same multi-finger capture applies to alternates. Separately, allow capturing a plain photo of the household head (and of each alternate) during registration -- explicitly *not* used for biometric verification, just so a registered person is visually identifiable later -- optional at registration time, resumable later, shown alongside the person's details.

**Findings from the existing codebase before writing any code:**
- `PersonCaptureActivity` is already the single, re-entrant capture screen used for *both* the household head and every alternate (reached from `HouseholdFormActivity`/`HouseholdDetailActivity`). It already calls `BiometricDevice.startCapture(fingerPosition, callback)` with a real `fingerPosition` parameter and `FingerprintDao` already has a `fingerprint_number` column -- but the activity hardcoded `startCapture(1, ...)` and `fingerNumber=1` on every capture, so multiple fingers were never actually reachable from the UI even though the data model already supported it.
- "At least one finger captured is enough to proceed" is *already* the effective rule (`fingerprintCaptured = fingerprintDao.countForBeneficiary(id) > 0`) -- no gating change needed there, only the missing per-finger picker UI.
- The backend (`Biometric.java` `ENROLL_FINGERPRINT`/sync paths) stores fingerprints keyed by `uuid` with no unique constraint on `(beneficiary, finger_number)` -- it already tolerates an unbounded list of fingerprint rows per beneficiary. No backend/migration change is needed for multi-finger capture.
- A full non-verification photo pipeline already exists end-to-end and is already unused by any capture screen: local `images` table + `ImageDao` (local path, sync status), `SyncManager.syncImages()` -> `UPLOAD_IMAGE`, backend `images` table + `imageUrls()`, and `GET_HOUSEHOLD`/alternate-list responses already return each beneficiary's `images` gallery (`Household.java` line ~1070 explicitly calls it a per-alternate gallery, ordered newest first). So "photo" is modeled server-side as a gallery, not a single slot -- decision: keep that model (never delete old photos), and always treat *the most recently captured image* as "the" display photo in the UI. Avoids any backend/schema change.
- No image-loading library (Glide/Coil) is in `build.gradle.kts` -- decided to hand-roll a small sampled-bitmap decode helper for thumbnails rather than add a dependency.

**Decisions made without the user available to confirm (flagged `[?]` below for a later check-in):**
- `[?]` Finger numbering: adopted the standard ANSI/NIST tenprint order 1-10 = Right Thumb, Right Index, Right Middle, Right Ring, Right Little, Left Thumb, Left Index, Left Middle, Left Ring, Left Little (stored in `fingerprints.fingerprint_number`). This is the conventional real-world layout (matches paper tenprint cards) rather than an invented scheme.
- `[?]` Hand picker is a static two-row (Right hand / Left hand) layout of 10 circular tap targets per person, each labelled with a short finger abbreviation, with a staggered vertical offset per finger (thumb/little shortest, middle tallest) to loosely read as a hand silhouette rather than a flat row. Default state = outlined; captured state = filled green (`bp_success`), per the request. Tapping an already-captured (green) finger asks to confirm before recapturing (replaces the old local template for that exact finger number; does not touch any other finger).
- `[?]` Photo capture reuses camera capture (back camera, no face-detection heuristics -- unlike the existing `FaceCaptureActivity`, this is just a plain photo) via a new lightweight `PhotoCaptureActivity`. Skippable at registration; the existing "Captured people" edit list and the read-only household profile screen are the two places it can be captured/recaptured later, matching how fingerprint/face capture already resume.
- `[?]` The photo is displayed (not just recorded) in three places: the `PersonCaptureActivity` capture screen itself, the household edit screen's "Captured people" row, and the read-only Household Profile screen (hero avatar for the head, per-row thumbnail for each member). Dashboard (`frontend/`) display of these photos was treated as out of scope for this pass -- it's a separate surface from "household registration" and the backend already exposes the data if that's wanted next.

**What was built:**
1. `FingerprintDao`: added `capturedFingerNumbers(beneficiaryId)` (drives the hand-picker's green/outlined state) and `deleteForBeneficiaryAndFinger(beneficiaryId, fingerNumber)` (recapture cleanly replaces that one finger's template instead of accumulating a duplicate row for the same finger).
2. `ImageDao`: added `latestLocalPathForBeneficiary(beneficiaryId)` (newest-first, matching the existing gallery model).
3. New `FingerPosition` (households package): the single source of truth for the 1-10 ANSI/NIST finger numbering, short/full labels, and hand groupings -- used by both the layout's content descriptions and `PersonCaptureActivity`'s Java.
4. New `PhotoCaptureActivity` + `activity_photo_capture.xml`: plain back-camera capture (no face detection/matching), registered in the manifest, returns a cache-file path like `FaceCaptureActivity` does.
5. New `ThumbnailLoader` (ui package): hand-rolled sampled-bitmap decode (no image-loading library in this project) used everywhere a captured photo is shown at small size.
6. `activity_person_capture.xml` rebuilt: the old single "Capture fingerprint" button is now a two-hand, 10-finger tap grid (`finger1`..`finger10`, right hand then left hand, ANSI/NIST order) with a staggered vertical offset per finger for a hand-silhouette read, backed by a new `bg_finger_circle` state selector (outlined -> green filled on capture) and `BioPay.FingerLabel`/`finger_label_color` for the in-circle abbreviation. Added a photo row (avatar thumbnail, status, capture/retake button) that's always visible regardless of registration method.
7. `PersonCaptureActivity` rewired: tapping a finger captures straight to that exact `fingerPosition` (already threaded through `BiometricDevice.startCapture`/vendor SDK -- no device-layer change needed); tapping an already-green finger confirms before recapturing; the status line reads "N of 10 fingers captured"; "at least one finger to proceed" already worked and needed no change. Photo capture launches `PhotoCaptureActivity`, moves the result from cache into persistent `filesDir/photos/`, and records it via `ImageDao` -- fully optional, resumable later, both for the household head and every alternate (same screen already served both).
8. Photo now displays (not just records) in three places: the capture screen itself, the household edit screen's "Captured people" row (`item_captured_person.xml` + `HouseholdFormActivity`, status text now also lists finger count and "Photo"), and the read-only Household Profile screen (`HouseholdDetailActivity` -- new hero avatar for the head, per-row circular thumbnail for every member in `item_household_member.xml`, falling back to the existing placeholder icon when no photo exists yet).
9. No backend or SQLite schema change was needed -- confirmed the server already tolerates an unbounded per-beneficiary fingerprint list keyed by `uuid` (not by finger number) and already has a full photo-gallery pipeline (`images` table, `UPLOAD_IMAGE`, `GET_HOUSEHOLD`'s `images`/`imageStatus`); `SyncManager` already pushes `fingerNumber` from the DAO rather than a hardcoded value, so multi-finger sync worked the moment the UI started writing real finger numbers.

**Verification:** Both `morphoSmart642` and `morphoSmart615` debug variants compiled cleanly (`compile*DebugJavaWithJavac`). `lintMorphoSmart642Debug` passed with zero findings touching any new/changed file. All unit tests passed for both flavors (`testMorphoSmart642DebugUnitTest`, `testMorphoSmart615DebugUnitTest`). `assembleMorphoSmart642Debug` produced a debug APK successfully. `git diff --check` reported only pre-existing LF/CRLF line-ending notices, no real whitespace errors.

**2026-09-08 (continued) -- real-device acceptance on the connected tablets:**
- Installed the rebuilt `morphoSmart642` debug APK on **MPH_MB003A** (serial `5041628786`). A second connected tablet, **MPH_MB001A** (serial `18420028`, `MorphoTablet2`), turned out to be running Android 5.0.2 (API 21) -- below this app's `minSdk 24` -- so `INSTALL_FAILED_OLDER_SDK` is a genuine hardware/OS ceiling, not a build problem; that device cannot run this app at all.
- On MPH_MB003A, driving the app via adb (signed in, opened an existing "Incomplete" household, exercised both new flows):
  - **Hand-picker confirmed working exactly as designed**: two-hand layout renders with the intended finger-length silhouette (Mid tallest, Th/Lt shortest), tap gives immediate visual (pressed-state) feedback, the fingerprint status line and "Not captured yet"/green-fill states are correct, and the screen survives a failed capture attempt with no orphaned/partial local row (confirmed by re-opening the screen afterward and seeing "Not captured yet" still, matching the delete-then-insert design).
  - **Photo capture confirmed working end-to-end on hardware**: `PhotoCaptureActivity` opens with a live back-camera preview, capture succeeds, the circular thumbnail renders immediately in the capture screen, the green "Photo captured" banner shows, the button relabels to "Retake photo", and the same photo correctly appears in both new display points -- the Household Profile screen's hero avatar (top of the green header) and its per-member row thumbnail (`item_household_member.xml`). No crash, no ANR.
  - **Live fingerprint scan itself could not be exercised.** Tapping a finger correctly calls into `BiometricDevice.open()`/`startCapture(fingerPosition, ...)` (confirmed via logcat: `MorphoDeviceAdapter`/`MORPHO_USB` initialization logs appear, tagged with the tapped finger's activity), but the embedded scanner never enumerated ("Android USB did not expose the embedded scanner before SDK initialization", "MORPHO_USB: Service not started") and no capture dialog/callback ever completed -- while the rest of the app stayed fully responsive (no ANR; other buttons kept working). This matches the **already-known constraint from the 2026-09-07/08 hardware sessions**: the embedded Morpho scanner and the tablet's external USB/adb debugging connection contend for the same USB path, and the scanner only works once that cable is disconnected. Nothing in this session's code changes touched that threading/USB-open path -- the single old "Capture fingerprint" button had the exact same synchronous `device.open()` call, so this is not a regression from the hand-picker rework.
- **What's still needed from the user**: unplug MPH_MB003A's USB cable (keeping it powered/on Wi-Fi) and manually tap through one real finger scan to confirm capture-to-green and the recapture-confirmation dialog end-to-end -- that's the one path this session's adb-driven testing structurally cannot reach. Everything else (layout, tap wiring, resume state, photo capture/display in all three locations) is now hardware-confirmed, not just build-verified.


### 2026-09-08 (continued) - Fixed the real capture crash, redesigned Household Profile around per-member capture

**User report after disconnecting USB and testing for real:** "it does not capture the app crashes and for the other fingers it does not even allow us to scan" -- plus explicit direction that household members should be tappable to expand and show their photo/captured fingerprints in place, and that scanning more fingers should happen from that expanded view per-person, replacing the household-wide "Capture" button paired with "Add alternate".

**Root cause of the crash**, found from the tablet's own crash log (`adb logcat -b crash`), timestamped ~3 minutes after the user took over from the handoff -- i.e. their actual live-scanner test:
```
java.lang.IllegalArgumentException: View=DecorView@...[PersonCaptureActivity] not attached to window manager
  at android.app.Dialog.dismiss
  at PersonCaptureActivity.lambda$checkDuplicateThenSave$1(PersonCaptureActivity.java:373)
```
`checkDuplicateThenSave` runs the offline duplicate-check on a background thread (a live scan plus that check can take several seconds), then calls `runOnUiThread(() -> { device.close(); dialog.dismiss(); ... })` unconditionally. If the activity's window was already torn down by the time that runnable ran -- backgrounding the app, screen lock, or a "don't keep activities"-style reclaim during the scan -- `dialog.dismiss()` throws and crashes the whole app. Once the app crashed, of course no further finger was scannable ("for the other fingers it does not even allow us to scan" was a symptom of the crash, not a second bug). This exact unsafe pattern pre-dated this session's changes (the original single "Capture fingerprint" button had the identical `runOnUiThread { ... dialog.dismiss() ... }` shape) -- it simply had never been exercised against a real, slow, live scan before now.
- Added `PersonCaptureActivity#isAliveForUi()` (`!isFinishing() && !isDestroyed()`) and guarded every UI touch that crosses a thread/async boundary with it: the duplicate-check's `dialog.dismiss()`/success/duplicate/error paths, the capture `onError` callback's `dialog.dismiss()`, and (same risk shape, hardened proactively since it's new code from earlier today) the photo-save and face-embedding background threads' `runOnUiThread` callbacks. None of these silently swallow real failures -- they only skip UI work once there is provably no window left to update.
- Confirmed no orphaned/partial state from the crash: after reinstalling the fix and reopening the same household, "0 Pending sync" and no stray fingerprint row for that person -- the crash happened after the duplicate-check but the `fingerprintDao.save()` call is further down in the same guarded block, so a torn-down activity now just drops that one capture attempt cleanly instead of crashing.

**Redesigned the Household Profile screen's capture entry point**, per explicit direction: each member row (`item_household_member.xml`, `HouseholdDetailActivity`) is now tappable and expands in place (chevron flips right/down) to show that specific person's captured-finger count, face status, and photo status, plus a **Capture** button that opens `PersonCaptureActivity` for exactly that person. This replaces the old household-wide **Capture** button, which -- on inspection while making this change -- turned out to only ever be able to reach the household head; there was previously no way at all to resume an alternate's capture from this read-only profile screen (only from inside the edit form's buried "Captured people" list). **Add alternate** remains, now full-width on its own row since it's still a distinct action (creating a new person, not capturing an existing one). The expanded member is remembered (`expandedBeneficiaryId`) across the round trip to `PersonCaptureActivity` and back, since the member list is fully rebuilt on every `onResume()` -- so returning from a capture lands back on the same person instead of a collapsed list.
- New `bg_surface_variant_panel` drawable for the expanded detail panel background; reused the existing `ic_chevron_right`/`ic_chevron_down` icons and the `person_capture_fingerprint_count`/`person_capture_captured`/`household_person_not_captured` strings already added earlier today rather than introducing new ones.

**Verification:** `compileMorphoSmart642DebugJavaWithJavac`/`compileMorphoSmart615DebugJavaWithJavac` passed, `lintMorphoSmart642Debug` passed with zero findings on any changed file, `testMorphoSmart642DebugUnitTest`/`testMorphoSmart615DebugUnitTest` passed, `assembleMorphoSmart642Debug` produced a debug APK. Reinstalled on MPH_MB003A and hardware-confirmed on the real device: the member row expands/collapses correctly, shows the right per-person status (fingerprint/face/photo), the **Capture** button opens `PersonCaptureActivity` scoped to that exact person (verified against both the head and would work identically for any alternate -- same code path), the existing photo still loads correctly into that flow, and the expanded state survives the round trip back from capture. The crash fix itself could not be re-triggered live in this pass (would need another real scan with the app deliberately backgrounded mid-scan to prove the exact race closes) -- the fix is verified by code review against the exact captured stack trace and by confirming the general `isAliveForUi()` guard compiles and doesn't regress the already-hardware-confirmed happy path.


### 2026-09-08 (continued) - Tap-to-expand full-screen photo viewer (mobile) + clickable photo lightbox (dashboard)

**User request:** "the photo that we capture can we not make it big or like we can click on it to expand it or like have its own place so we can see it clearly and in the dashboard when we view a household we should be able to see the photo also."

**Mobile:** Added `com.biopay.agent.ui.PhotoViewerActivity` -- a generic full-screen photo viewer (black background, dark toolbar, `fitCenter`) opened from any small photo thumbnail already in the app. `ThumbnailLoader.makeExpandable(ImageView, path, hasPhoto)` centralizes the wiring (skips binding a click when the placeholder icon is showing, since there's nothing to expand) and is now called everywhere a captured photo is displayed: `PersonCaptureActivity`'s capture-screen thumbnail (also bumped 56dp -> 72dp for a better default glance), `HouseholdDetailActivity`'s hero avatar and each member row's small photo, and `HouseholdFormActivity`'s "Captured people" row. All four gained a `selectableItemBackground` ripple foreground so they read as tappable. The member-row photo (38dp, nested inside the row's own expand/collapse click target) intentionally splits the interaction: tapping the tiny avatar opens the photo, tapping elsewhere on the row still expands/collapses the detail panel -- confirmed on-device this doesn't conflict.
- **Found while investigating the dashboard side of this request**: the dashboard (`frontend/src/pages/HouseholdDetailPage.vue`, the actual per-household page -- not the `HouseholdsPage.vue` list I'd checked in an earlier pass) already fetched and displayed household/alternate photos (a "Photos" card plus alternate avatars/gallery, each blob-fetched through the authenticated `apiClient` since the JWT-protected `/files/:filename` route can't be reached from a plain `<img src>`). That earlier progress.md note about dashboard photo display being "out of scope for this pass" was therefore based on looking at the wrong page -- corrected here rather than left stale.
- What was actually missing, matching the same "big / click to expand" request: none of those thumbnails were clickable, and the household head's own photo had no prominent placement near the header (only buried in the same gallery card as any other uploaded photos). Added a 56px header avatar (the most recent household photo) next to the household name, and a shared `lightboxSrc` ref + `v-dialog` (reusing the existing global `DialogCloseButton` component) that any photo -- header avatar, household gallery, alternate avatar, alternate gallery -- opens on click, showing it large with `contain` scaling.
