### 2026-09-09 (continued) - Mobile reports, household defaults, More logout, and compact Home summary

**User report:** the mobile report surfaces were rounder than the rest of the product; choosing Today still appeared to show lifetime data; growing summaries such as registrations by location needed a scalable presentation; logout belonged at the bottom of More instead of Settings; household defaults and gender capture needed correction; the literacy question was unclear; and the Home Operational summary needed to be slightly smaller.

- **Report filtering:** `reports/ReportDao.java` now builds an explicit local-time window with both an inclusive start and exclusive end. Today is exactly the current local calendar day; 7-day and 30-day ranges have matching bounded windows; All remains unbounded. Activity totals, payment status/amounts, biometric readiness, incomplete records, registrations by location, recent activity, and chart aggregation use the selected window. The pending-sync count intentionally remains an all-device operational state. The screen now states the active scope and changes the chart heading between Activity today, Activity over the last 7 days, and Recent 7-day activity so a short chart is not mistaken for an all-time total.
- **Scalable report cards:** the report summary shows only the top five registration locations. A View all locations action opens `ReportLocationsActivity`, backed by a `RecyclerView`, so a large number of locations is rendered as a virtualized scrolling list instead of expanding the main report indefinitely. Recent activity was already capped at five with its existing View all activity action; the activity/payment/readiness sections are fixed-size aggregates and do not grow by record count.
- **Flatter report geometry:** added a report-specific 6dp surface radius and applied the existing 8dp control radius to the range selector. The device-scope strip no longer uses a 50dp pill background. This aligns Reports with the system's restrained card/control geometry.
- **Logout placement:** removed logout and its dialog/session code from Settings. The same confirmed session-clearing logout action now sits below the More menu card, at the bottom of the page.
- **Household registration:** new households default Legal status to Citizen, and the ambiguous Not recorded option was removed. A legacy record with a missing status is not silently converted; it must be resolved before the next save. Head gender now starts unselected and saving requires Male or Female.
- **Alternate gender assistance:** selecting an unambiguous relationship now proposes the corresponding gender (for example Son -> Male and Daughter -> Female) in both initial person capture and alternate editing. Ambiguous relationships such as Cousin clear the field and require a manual choice; gender remains editable in all cases. Added `RelationshipGenderTest` coverage for male, female, and ambiguous relationships.
- **Eligibility/support wording:** changed Household head is literate to Household head is illiterate. Illiteracy is the support/vulnerability condition being captured, so checking the box now stores `literate=N`, while leaving it unchecked stores `literate=Y`; edit mode maps existing data back correctly.
- **Home sizing:** reduced Operational summary padding, mini-card height from 108dp to 96dp, KPI text from 20sp to 18sp, and excess vertical spacing. The four-card horizontal structure and color language remain unchanged.

**Verification:** both `compileMorphoSmart615DebugJavaWithJavac` and `compileMorphoSmart642DebugJavaWithJavac` passed; both complete flavor unit-test suites passed; both debug APKs assembled successfully; the Impeccable detector returned no findings for the five changed mobile layouts; and `git diff --check` reported no whitespace errors. Installed the matching APKs with data preserved on connected devices `18420028` (morpho615) and `5041628786` (morpho642), both successfully. Live-checking the 642 tablet confirmed the smaller Operational summary, the logout action at the bottom of More, no logout in Settings, flatter Reports surfaces, explicit Today/All scope copy, blank initial head gender, unchecked Household head is illiterate, and Citizen as the default legal status. The current test device's ten households and seven payments were all recorded today, so Today and All correctly have equal numeric totals on that dataset; the different scope labels and the bounded query implementation were confirmed.

### 2026-09-09 (continued) - Mobile landscape: search-list screens (household in particular) were mostly header, one row visible at a time

**User report:** on the 6.15 flavor in landscape, the app doesn't use the available space -- not edge to edge either direction -- and on a screen like Households, roughly three quarters of the height is the title/search/filter header plus the bottom nav bar, leaving room for a single household row; you have to scroll one at a time.

Root cause was the shared search-list header pattern (`MaterialToolbar` with a title, then a duplicate `BioPay.ScreenTitle` + `BioPay.SupportingText` restating that same title, a hardcoded 56dp `SearchView`, and a filter-chip row) sized for portrait's spare vertical room, plus a single-column `LinearLayoutManager` that only ever showed one card per row regardless of how much width landscape actually had. Six screens share this exact structure: Households, Alternates, Attendance, Attendance beneficiaries, Payment verification, Voucher redemption, and the Activity feed.

- **`HouseholdListActivity.java`** (the screen named in the report, given the fullest fix): in landscape (`Configuration.ORIENTATION_LANDSCAPE`), hides the redundant `tvScreenTitle`/`tvScreenDescription` pair (the toolbar already carries the title) and swaps the RecyclerView's `LinearLayoutManager` for a 2-column `GridLayoutManager`, so two households render per row instead of one. `activity_household_list.xml` gained ids on those two views for the visibility toggle and now sources its search bar height/margin and filter-row margin from dimens instead of hardcoded values.
- **`BioPay.ScreenTitle` style** (`values/styles.xml`) now takes its `textSize` from `@dimen/screen_title_text_size` instead of a hardcoded `24sp` -- this alone shrinks every screen's duplicated title in landscape (24sp -> 18sp), not just Households, with no per-layout changes needed.
- **New dimens** (`values/dimens.xml` defaults unchanged; `values-land/dimens.xml` overrides, same established pattern as `login_logo_max_height`): `list_header_top_padding` (8dp -> 4dp), `list_search_height` (56dp -> 44dp), `list_search_margin_top` (16dp -> 8dp), `list_filter_margin_top` (12dp -> 6dp). Wired into the search bar and header padding of `activity_household_list.xml`, `activity_alternates.xml`, `activity_attendance.xml`, `activity_attendance_beneficiaries.xml`, `activity_voucher_redemption.xml`, and `activity_activity_feed.xml`. `activity_payment_verification.xml` only got the header `paddingTop` swap -- its search bar sits lower in a longer, non-paginated confirmation screen rather than being the main scroll blocker, so shrinking it wasn't the same priority.
- Only Households got the title-hide + grid-layout treatment (the two changes that need activity code, not just a dimen). The other five screens' `ScreenTitle`/`SupportingText` still render in landscape, now smaller, since resolving that fully within scope would have meant repeating the same id-and-visibility-toggle wiring six times for a report that named one screen specifically.

**Verification:** `:app:compileMorphoSmart615DebugJavaWithJavac` and `:app:processMorphoSmart615DebugResources` both passed clean (Gradle task names confirmed via `:app:tasks --all` first -- "615"/"642" turned out to be the `morphoSmart615`/`morphoSmart642` product flavor names, i.e. the two supported fingerprint-scanner models, not a screen-size figure). **Not done:** no emulator/device was available in this session to visually confirm the landscape result on-screen -- worth a real landscape check on a 6.15 tablet next time one's connected, and worth deciding whether Alternates/Attendance/etc. should get the same title-hide + grid treatment as Households or are fine with just the smaller header.

### 2026-09-09 (continued) - Root-caused the recurring clipped-field-label bug in dialogs (not the Ubuntu-ascender fix from before)

**User report:** a floating field label ("Access scope" specifically) still renders with its top cut off in a popup, despite this having been "reported fixed" before -- asked to check every field so it stops recurring, rather than patching this one instance again.

**Found the actual root cause via live browser inspection (not another guess from the CSS alone), and it's a different bug from the one `ui-fixes.css`'s existing `.v-label.v-field-label--floating` rule already fixes.** Reproduced live on the Roles & Permissions "Edit role" dialog (`admin@biopay.com`, TOTP login), then confirmed via `getBoundingClientRect()`/computed-style inspection in the actual page:
- Every dialog's scrollable body is either a `.v-card-text` or a raw `<form>` (a direct `.v-card` child), and `style.css` gives both `overflow-y: auto` (`.v-overlay[role='dialog'] .v-card-text` / `.v-overlay[role='dialog'] .v-card > form`, added for the viewport-safe-dialog-scrolling work) -- with **zero top padding of its own**. Each dialog's `-editor`-suffixed card class (`role-editor`, `org-editor`, etc.) puts its padding on the outer `.v-card`, not on this inner scrolling element.
- A field's floating label legitimately paints a few pixels *above* its own field box by design (that's the classic Material "notch" look). Measured on the clipped "Access scope" field: label top `94.4px` vs. its scrolling container's own top edge at `102.8px` -- the label overshot the container's `overflow: auto` clip boundary by `8.4px` and got sheared off.
- **Why it looked "fixed" before and then came back**: whether that overshoot actually clips depends entirely on how much incidental space happens to sit above the first field -- a section header with an icon (e.g. Organizations' "Organization details" label) happens to add just enough clearance, while Roles & Permissions' edit dialog puts "Role name"/"Access scope" immediately after the subtitle with nothing else above them, so it clips. Confirmed by testing both live: the Organizations "New Organization" dialog's first row (`Anchor`, floated) does **not** clip; Roles & Permissions' first row does. Same underlying CSS structure, different amount of luck. Patching one dialog's spacing would only move the goalpost to whichever dialog is first-row-tight next.
- **Fix**: added `padding-top: 10px` to both `.v-overlay[role='dialog'] .v-card-text` and `.v-overlay[role='dialog'] .v-card > form` in `style.css` -- moves the clip boundary itself up and away from any field's float, everywhere, permanently, rather than depending on each dialog's own incidental spacing above its first row. 10px clears the measured 8.4px overshoot with margin for other field densities.
- **Verified live**, not just visually re-read: re-opened the same Roles & Permissions "Edit wazito" dialog after the fix -- "Role name" and "Access scope" both render with fully intact ascenders (zoomed screenshot comparison, before/after). Also opened Organizations' "New Organization" and Users' "Add user" dialogs (both raw-`<form>` dialogs, `Access scope` and `Anchor` as literal first fields) to confirm the extra padding doesn't look wrong or break anything there -- it doesn't, it just adds a small even margin above every dialog's content that was previously inconsistent dialog-to-dialog.

**Verification:** `cd frontend && npm run build` (`vue-tsc -b && vite build`) clean, all existing chunks unaffected. No test data left behind -- a "Test Org" typed into the New Organization dialog while reproducing this was never submitted (dialog closed via Escape, not Create).

**Also completed, same session: the login now uses TOTP instead of email OTP** for `admin@biopay.com`, per the user's correction -- the account already had TOTP enrolled (`TOTP_SETUP_INIT` in `Auth.java`, unrelated to this session's work), it just needed the user to enter the live code themselves once in the shared browser session; no code change was needed for this part.

### 2026-09-10 - Provisioned two field devices and root-caused a chain of bugs blocking all fingerprint capture

**Context:** user connected two physical field devices and asked to install the matching agent app and make sure the dashboard's downloadable APKs were current. Turned into a full-session debugging chain once the devices actually tried to use their fingerprint scanners -- five separate real bugs, found and fixed one at a time as each fix exposed the next symptom underneath it.

**Device identification and initial provisioning:**
- Device 1: `adb devices -l` showed a Morpho tablet (serial `18420028`, product `MorphoTablet2`, model `MPH_MB001A`). `getprop` confirmed Android 5.0.2 / SDK 21, manufacturer IDEMIA -- matches the **morphoSmart615** flavor (Android 5.0+ minimum; the 642 flavor requires SDK 24+ and Android refuses to install it on this device at all).
- Device 2: serial `5041628786`, product `ID_Screen_N`, model `MPH-MB003A`, Android 10 / SDK 29, arm64 -- matches **morphoSmart642** ("IDEMIA embedded scanner (SDK 6.42)").
- Found `backend/app-releases/*.apk` were stale (source under `mobile/agent/` had commits newer than the last build). Rebuilt both flavors and copied them into `backend/app-releases/` (the dashboard's Android App page and `/biopay/downloads/:filename` route serve straight from this folder, per its own README) -- repeated after every fix below, so the dashboard always carried the same build installed on the test devices by the end of the session.
- The project has no release signing config; the previously-deployed "release" APKs were actually signed with the debug key (confirmed via `keytool -printcert` on `META-INF/CERT.RSA`, issuer "Android Debug"). Matched that existing scheme by using the debug build variant throughout rather than introducing real release signing (out of scope for this session).

**Bug 1 -- Settings screen crashed and silently fell back to Home (`SettingsActivity`, both flavors).**
- `adb logcat` on the crash caught it directly: `InflateException` -> `ArrayIndexOutOfBoundsException: length=54; index=1497` in `StringBlock.get`, while inflating a `MaterialButton`. Android's crash-recovery silently returned to whatever activity was behind it (Home), which is why it looked like a navigation bug rather than a crash.
- A clean `./gradlew clean` rebuild did **not** fix it (ruled out stale incremental-build resources). Isolated the exact trigger with an A/B test: the identical APK opened Settings fine on the Android 10 device but crashed on the Android 5.0.2 one -- confirmed this is a real **Android 5.0/5.1 OS bug** decoding `MaterialButton`'s `app:icon` attribute under `Widget.Material3.Button.TextButton`, not a resource-build issue.
- **Fix**: switched every affected button from `app:icon` to plain `android:drawableStart`/`drawableEnd` (which uses `TextView`'s native compound-drawable path instead of `MaterialButton`'s icon-rendering code) -- confirmed by isolated single-button test (crash line moved from the first affected button to the next one down, then disappeared once all were converted). Applied across `activity_settings.xml` (3 buttons), `activity_generate_payment.xml`, and `activity_reports.xml` (3 buttons) -- same style+icon combination, same latent crash risk on old-Android field tablets, even though only Settings had been exercised yet.

**Bug 2 -- 615 tablet's fingerprint scanner silently never became available (`MorphoDeviceAdapter`, morphoSmart615 only).**
- Settings' scanner status flipped from "Connected" to permanently "Not connected" with no visible error, across every retry. `adb logcat` showed `art: Rejecting re-init on previously-failed class java.lang.Class<com.morpho.android.usb.USBManager>` -- ART permanently blacklists a class after its static initializer throws once per process, and the vendor SDK's native `.so` load happens in that static initializer.
- Root cause: the flavor's native libraries (`libMSO100.so`, `libNativeMorphoSmartSDK_6.15.3.0.so`, `libserial_port.so`, `libmupdf.so`) were packaged only under `jniLibs/armeabi/` (the legacy 32-bit ABI folder). The same APK also ships `armeabi-v7a` binaries (onnxruntime/face-detector, for the face-recognition feature), so Android's installer picked `armeabi-v7a` as this device's sole primary ABI and never touched the `armeabi` folder at all -- confirmed via `pm dump` showing `primaryCpuAbi=armeabi-v7a`, `secondaryCpuAbi=null`. The Morpho `.so` files were never extracted onto the device.
- **Fix**: duplicated the same four `.so` files into a new `jniLibs/armeabi-v7a/` directory alongside the existing `armeabi` one. Confirmed in the rebuilt APK (`unzip -l`) and live on-device (native library loaded without error, no more "Rejecting re-init").

**Bug 3 -- fingerprint capture and the duplicate-check step both silently failed on 615, even with the scanner "Connected."**
- Decompiled `MorphoSmart_SDK_6.15.3.0.jar` (`javap`) and found `MorphoDevice.initUsbDevicesNameEnum`/`verifyMatch` take a `java.lang.Integer` the native code mutates **in place** as an out-parameter (an old JNI reflection hack) -- and the SDK's own log line spelled out the fix: `E/MORPHO_USB: Error, initialize Integer with new Integer(0)`. Our code passed a literal `0`, which Java autoboxes to the cached `Integer.valueOf(0)` singleton; the SDK refuses to mutate that shared instance.
- **Fix**: changed every call site (`isAvailable()`, `open()`, `templatesMatch()`) to pass `new Integer(0)` instead of the autoboxed/cached literal. Also added a 40-attempt/250ms retry loop around enumeration (matching the 642 adapter's existing pattern, since USB permission grants are asynchronous) and added the `Log.e` this adapter's `isAvailable()` catch block was missing (present in the 642 adapter, absent here -- the gap that made bug 2 hard to diagnose in the first place).

**Bug 4 -- the very first capture attempt on a fresh scanner always failed, on both flavors (`PersonCaptureActivity`, `VoucherRedemptionActivity`, `AttendanceBeneficiariesActivity`).**
- All three called `device.open()` **synchronously on the UI thread**. That method blocks for several seconds retrying USB enumeration, and on first use of a given USB device Android must show its own "Allow app to access USB device?" system permission dialog and wait for a tap -- but the UI thread was busy blocking in the retry loop, starving that permission flow, so the first attempt always timed out. `FingerprintVerifyActivity` already did this correctly on a background `ExecutorService`, with its own comment stating exactly why ("must never run on Android's UI thread") -- the other three screens just hadn't been built to match.
- **Fix**: added a single-thread `ExecutorService` to each of the three activities, moved `device.open()` onto it, and made the capture/verify dialog show immediately with a "Connecting to fingerprint scanner..." message while that runs, wiring a `cancelled` flag through so a mid-open Cancel tap doesn't proceed into a stale capture. Added executor shutdown in each activity's `onDestroy()`.

**Bug 5 -- 615 tablet's scanner stopped responding entirely ("won't turn on") after the above fixes.**
- A full device reboot fixed it -- a firmware power-rail state issue, not a code bug. Checked first whether the 642 flavor's `powerOnFingerprintPeripherals()` AIDL power-on mechanism (needed on newer ID Screen tablets, which default the sensor's power rail off) had an equivalent on this device's older firmware: `dumpsys package` found no matching IDEMIA peripheral service anywhere on the device, and the 6.15.3.0 SDK's full public API (`javap`) exposes no software power-on call at all. No code lever exists for this on this device/SDK combination -- correctly left as a hardware/firmware issue, not chased further in code.

**Bug 6 -- the real headline bug: capture worked for some fingers (thumb) but failed for others, and every left-hand finger failed instantly on 615 with no scan attempt at all.**
- Isolated with a clean test (fresh app process, left-hand finger tapped *first*, before any right-hand attempt) to separate "wrong value for this specific finger" from "corrupted state from a prior attempt": left thumb failed in under two seconds with **"Capture problem: Error code -5"** -- reproduced live via `adb` on a "Test" household already present on the device (no real beneficiary data touched). Decompiled `ErrorCodes.class`: `-5 = MORPHOERR_BADPARAMETER`.
- **Root cause, confirmed against both vendor SDKs' own original pre-refactor reference code still sitting in this repo** (`mobile/nca/.../MorphoTabletFPSensorDevice.java` for 615, `mobile/dca/.../FingerprintsActivity.java` for 642 -- both explicitly named in this adapter's own class javadoc as its porting source): `MorphoDevice.capture()`'s 4th parameter is **not** a finger-position selector at all. Both original references hardcode it to the literal `1` (the dca reference's own comment: *"Here fingerNumber = 1, so we will get only one template"*) -- it's a template-count parameter. This app's port had mistakenly threaded the app's own 1-10 finger-position value into that slot instead. The 615 SDK strictly validates it and rejects anything above 5 outright (explaining every left-hand finger failing instantly); the 642 SDK is more lenient and didn't crash, but was still receiving the wrong value for whatever it actually controls, plausibly explaining that flavor's own earlier, vaguer "capture doesn't work right" reports.
- **Fix**: changed the 4th argument of `morphoDevice.capture(...)` from `fingerPosition` to the literal `1` in **both** `MorphoDeviceAdapter` implementations (615 and 642), with a comment on each citing the matching original reference file. The app's own finger-position tracking (dialog title, `fingerprintDao.save(..., fingerPosition, ...)` labeling) is entirely separate code and was never affected.
- **Verified live**: same "Test" household, fresh app process, left thumb tapped -- dialog now shows "Left thumb / Place finger for acquisition" and genuinely waits on the sensor instead of rejecting in under two seconds. User then confirmed a full retest: **"tested and it captured all fingers"**, both hands, both tablets.

**State left in:** both `backend/app-releases/*.apk` and both physical devices are on the final build containing all six fixes. No backend or frontend changes this session -- entirely `mobile/agent/` (both product flavor source sets) plus the two native `.so` files duplicated into `armeabi-v7a`.
